_source line 721 · 2026-05-18T14:36:32.593159+00:00_

33	\begin{abstract}
34	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
35	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable) using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
36	Across five architecture families (1B--14B), three source domains, and four decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for general web text (8/9 model configurations) and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for structured academic text (arXiv).
37	A single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \emph{one-wash laundering} effect.
38	Generator-independent statistical features, computed via a fixed reference scorer, enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among continuation-mode generators, and cross-scorer validation is consistent with $\Kempirical$ being governed by the generation process rather than the scoring model.
39	Downstream, domain-appropriate $\Kempirical$ correctly predicts optimal curation granularity: when contamination causes detectable quality loss, binary filtering minimizes degradation most effectively for web text.
40	\end{abstract}

27	\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}
28	For web text, 2v3 $\auc < \theta$ across all model configurations, so depth assignments for $d \geq 2$ approximate random labels; graduated weighting injects noise, explaining convergence toward binary at high $\alpha$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
29	The strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with the $\Kempirical{=}2$ prediction: graduated approaches cannot exploit depth information that does not exist beyond the discrimination boundary.
30	On arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement over unfiltered data, consistent with $\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.
31	DSIR fails differently: bigram hashing captures domain shift but not depth-correlated signatures (pattern persists across 10--50\% contamination ratios; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
32	
33	\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}
34	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia and OLMo match the 15D boundary at $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}); the 15D features trade one depth level for cross-model consistency (8/9 configurations $\Kempirical{\leq}2$).

4	\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}
5	Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model}.
6	In the context of diffusion models, \citet{shi2025collapse} trace a generalization-to-memorization transition under iterative synthetic training.
7	\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} propose surplexity, a single surprisal-based collapse indicator; our 15-dimensional feature set captures richer distributional structure and enables cross-model transfer.
8	\citet{amin2025escaping} show that weak-data curation can mitigate collapse, connecting to our filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
9	Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}, making $\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.
10	This literature characterizes macro dynamics but provides no per-text depth estimation, the gap we address.
11	
12	\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
13	Binary detectors~\cite{gehrmann2019gltr, mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, hans2024binoculars, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate.

line 30 的实验结果否定了这一点。因此 \"K* predicts curation granularity\" 在 non-trivial 条件下被证伪。\n\n**Abstract 需要修改：** \"domain-appropriate K* correctly predicts optimal curation granularity\" 应改为更诚实的表述，如 \"K*=2 [Round 1 审稿请求]:
## Round 1 审稿任务 — Reviewer 1（方法严谨性 + 实验有效性）

### 目标 Venue
- **EMNLP 2026**（通过 ARR May 2026 提交）
- 截止日期：2026-05-25
- Long paper：8 页正文 + 无限引用
- 模板：ACL LaTeX，`\usepackage[review]{acl}`
- Double-blind
- Limitations / Ethics 不计入页数
- ARR Overall Assessment 评分：5=Award, 4=Conference, 3=Findings, 2=Resubmit, 1=Do not resubmit

### 论文路径
`projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/main.tex`
（包含 introduction.tex, related_work.tex, method.tex, experiments_c1.tex, experiments_c3.tex, discussion.tex, conclusion.tex, appendix.tex, appendix_extended.tex）

### 你的审查侧重

**方法严谨性**：
1. K* 的形式化定义（Eq. 1）是否严密？θ=0.60 的选择是否有充分依据？
2. OBD-LightGBM 分类器设计是否合理？与替代方案的比较是否充分？
3. 15D 特征集的选择是否有理论或实验支撑？self_BLEU 的 leakage 问题是否真正解决？
4. Bootstrap CI 和 permutation test 的统计方法是否正确使用？样本量是否充足？
5. Cross-scorer validation 的设计和解读是否合理？

**实验有效性**：
6. 9 model configurations 的选择是否有代表性？Qwen 家族 5/9 的比重是否导致 bias？
7. C4/arXiv/Wikipedia 三个 domain 的实验设计是否支撑 "domain as sole dominant factor" 的 claim？
8. Filtering 实验（Section 5）的设置和统计显著性是否支撑 C3 claim？100% synthetic 是否限制了结论的泛化性？
9. Cross-model transfer 的结论是否过度推广（特别是 80-92% retention 的 claim）？
10. 边界案例（Mistral K*=1, Qwen-cont marginal, LLaMA borderline）的处理是否诚实透明？

请出具标准审稿报告：Summary → Strengths → Weaknesses → Questions → Missing References → Detailed Comments → Overall Assessment（使用 ARR 评分标准）。

32	% === Abstract ===
33	\begin{abstract}
34	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
35	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable) using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
36	Across five architecture families (1B--14B), three source domains, and four decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for general web text (8/9 model configurations) and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for structured academic text (arXiv).
37	A single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \emph{one-wash laundering} effect.
38	Largely generator-independent statistical features, computed via a fixed reference scorer, enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among continuation-mode generators, and cross-scorer validation confirms that $\Kempirical$ is largely robust to scorer choice.
39	Downstream, $\Kempirical$ characterizes discriminability boundaries that inform, but do not directly determine, filtering

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across multiple communities: binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}), membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
11	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
12	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
13	
14	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
15	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choi
…[truncated 2135 chars]

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}
5	Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model}.
6	In the context of diffusion models, \citet{shi2025collapse} trace a generalization-to-memorization transition under iterative synthetic training.
7	\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} propose surplexity, a single surprisal-based collapse indicator; our 15-dimensional feature set captures richer distributional structure and enables cross-model transfer.
8	\citet{amin2025escaping} show that weak-data curation can mitigate collapse, connecting to our filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
9	Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}, making $\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.
10	This literature characterizes macro dynamics but provides no per-text depth estimation, the gap we address.
11	
12	\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
13	Binary detectors~\cite{gehrmann2019gltr, mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, hans2024binoculars, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate.
14	Recent work extends beyond binary labels to collaboration degree~\cite{cheng2025beyond}, contamination hierarchies~\cite{mehta2025beyond}, and multigenerator benchmarks~\cite{wang2024semeval}; robustness concerns arise under paraphrasing~\cite{sadasivan2023detect, krishna2023paraphrasing} and cross-mode transfer~\cite{hao2025rewrite}; CoDeC~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses contamination via membership inference.
15	None estimates recursive generation depth.
16	Watermarking~\cite{kirchenbauer2023watermark} embeds d

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
7	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
8	\label{fig:pipeline}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
12	\label{sec:formulation}
13	
14	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
16	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
17	Together with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable.
18	
19	We define the operational lower bound:
20	\begin{equation}
21	  \Kempirical = \max\{k \geq 1 : \auc_{k-1,k} > \theta\},
22	  \label{eq:kstar}
23	\end{equation}
24	where $\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) and $\theta = 0.60$ is a data-driven operational threshold (motivated below).
25	Bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals (CIs; 1{,}000 resamples) provide uncertainty quantification: we verify that boundary-pair CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ and non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
26	
27	\paragraph{Threshold choice.}
28	We set $\theta{=}0.60$ as a data-driven operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
29	A 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\,0.55$ and $p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons, placing $\theta{=}0.60$ well above the empirical null.
30	$\Kempirical{=}2$

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate generational depth estimation on nine model configurations spanning five architecture families:
5	Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-14B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B, all in continuation mode except where noted.
6	All experiments use the OBD-LightGBM classifier with the 15-dimensional feature set (Section~\ref{sec:method}), with 5{,}000 texts per depth level (generations 0--5).

1	\section{Downstream Filtering Validation}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	We test whether $\Kempirical$ has predictive value for curation strategy selection, using a single-model setup (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct) as proof of concept.
5	$\Kempirical = 2$ predicts that binary curation should suffice, and that graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.
6	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\textbf{graduated} ($w = \exp(-\alpha \cdot \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), (v)~\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\textbf{DSIR}~\citep{xie2023dsir}.
7	We fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via LoRA (rank~8, $\alpha_{\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU (a multitask language understanding benchmark) and HellaSwag (a commonsense reasoning benchmark) across 5 seeds (51 total runs: 5 base strategies $\times$ 5 seeds $+$ 5 graduated-$\alpha$ values $\times$ 5 seeds $+$ 1 base-model reference; this 100\% synthetic setting is an upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
8

Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}

17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
19	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
20	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
21	
22	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure the generational depth discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.
5	$\Kempirical \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ depends on source domain and model characteristics: general web text yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ across 8/9 configurations (5 families, 1B--14B; $p \leq 0.002$), with $\Kempirical{=}3$ for academic text, confirmed across five models and four architecture families.
6	Generator-independent features enable 80--92\% accuracy retention across generators ($\rho > 0.90$), making $\Kempirical$ measurement practical without generator-specific training.
7	As downstream validation, $\Kempirical{=}2$ supports binary filtering as the optimal granularity for web text: graduated depth weighting cannot exploit information that does not exist beyond the discrimination boundary.
8	$\Kempirical$ provides a representation-dependent lower bound on discriminable depth; the gap to $\Ktheory$ may narrow with richer features, scaling beyond 14B, and retrain-chain characterization.
9

# ACL Rolling Review Form - Scoring Dimensions

## 1. **Reviewer Confidence**
Scale: 1-5
- 5: "Positive that my evaluation is correct"
- 4: "Quite sure" with unlikely but possible errors
- 3: "Pretty sure" but may have missed details
- 2: "Willing to defend" but likely missed some points
- 1: Outside expertise area or incomprehensible paper

## 2. **Soundness**
Scale: 1-5 (with .5 increments)
- 5: "Excellent: most thorough study of its type"
- 4: "Strong: sufficient support for all claims"
- 3: "Acceptable: sufficient support for main claims"
- 2: "Poor: main claims not sufficiently supported"
- 1: "Major Issues: not sufficiently thorough for publication"

## 3. **Excitement**
Scale: 1-5 (with .5 increments)
Subjective assessment based on innovation, impact, or relevance:
- 5: "Highly Exciting: would recommend to others"
- 4: "Exciting: would mention to others"
- 3: "Interesting: might mention some points"
- 2: "Potentially Interesting: doesn't resonate personally"
- 1: "Not Exciting: irrelevant to computational linguistics"

## 4. **Overall Assessment**
Scale: 1-5 (with .5 increments)
- 5: "Consider for Award" (top 2.5%)
- 4: "Conference" acceptance
- 3: "Findings" acceptance
- 2: "Resubmit next cycle"
- 1: "Do not resubmit"

## 5. **Reproducibility**
Scale: 1-5
- 5: Results easily reproducible
- 4: Mostly reproducible with minor variations
- 3: Reproducible with difficulty
- 2: Hard to reproduce; limited data availability
- 1: Cannot reproduce results

## 6. **Datasets** (if applicable)
Scale: 1-5
- 5: "Enabling" for research directions
- 4: "Useful" for ongoing work
- 3: "Potentially useful"
- 2: "Documentary" value only
- 1: No usable datasets

## 7. **Software** (if applicable)
Scale: 1-5
- 5: "Enabling" for research directions
- 4: "Useful" for ongoing work
- 3: "Potentially useful"
- 2: "Documentary" value only
- 1: No usable software

_source line 769 · 2026-05-18T15:14:29.329068+00:00_

# ARR Reviewer Guidelines Summary

## Core Evaluation Dimensions

ARR reviewers assess papers using three scores:

1. **Soundness**: Technical correctness and methodological rigor. Must be justified by specific review content. Low soundness scores require clear explanations of identified flaws.

2. **Excitement**: Personal interest level, orthogonal to soundness. Reflects subjective taste without requiring explicit justification.

3. **Overall Assessment**: Composite recommendation considering soundness, excitement, novelty, and potential impact. Papers for main conferences must be "sufficiently sound and reproducible."

## What Constitutes a Good Review

Reviewers should be **specific and actionable**. Rather than stating "X is not clear," explain "Y and Z are missing from the description of X." Vague criticisms like "missing relevant references" should become "missing references [specific citations]."

Reviews must maintain **professional, neutral tone**. The guidelines emphasize: "Write the review you would like to get yourself." Sarcasm and dismissiveness harm authors' mental health and undermine the peer review system.

## Key Evaluation Standards

**Critical Tasks:**

- Read papers twice: initial skim to understand research questions, then in-depth analysis
- Check assignments for expertise match and conflicts of interest
- Flag desk-rejection issues: anonymity violations, style guide breaches, hallucinated citations, missing limitations sections
- Assess reproducibility: sufficient implementation details, code/data availability plans

**Common Review Pitfalls to Avoid:**

The guidelines list 17 heuristics (H1-H17) that often lead to unfair criticism:

- Dismissing results as "not surprising" (hindsight bias)
- Rejecting non-SOTA work when SOTA isn't the paper's claim
- Treating negative results as weaknesses rather than contributions
- Criticizing simplicity when elegance is preferable
- Demanding comparisons to closed-source models without justification
- Li

位置：Section 3.1 (Eq. 1, \"Threshold choice\" paragraph), Figure 3\n- 论文声称 θ=0.60 是 \"data-driven operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution\"。但这个 discontinuity 正是 1v2 AUC (0.61-0.70) 和 2v3 AUC (0.55-0.59) 之间的间隔——阈值被选在恰好分开这两个 cluster 的位置，然后用此阈值定义 K*=2。尽管 permutation test (p < 0.002) 和 sensitivity analysis (K*=2 stable in [0.575, 0.625]) 提供了部分支持，但该窗口仅 5pp 宽：θ=0.55 使两个 Qwen 配置升至 K*=3，θ=0.625 以上 OLMo 降至 K*=1。\n- 建议：(1) 提供基于外部标准的阈值理由；(2) 将 θ 作为参数报告 K* 范围而非固定单一值；(3) 讨论 selection bias——先看数据分布再选阈值需要 held-out validation。\n\n**N2** [critical] DPI 论证到有限 K* 的跳跃缺乏严密性\n\n- 位置：Section 3.1 (\"A generation operator G_δ...\" paragraph)\n- DPI 保证 I(X; Y_k) 单调减少，但 pairwise AUC_{k-1,k} 度量的是相邻两代之间的可区分性，与 I(X; Y_k) 是不同的量。从 \"information degrades monotonically\" 到 \"finite K*\" 需要 G_δ contraction rate 有下界等额外假设。论文在 Discussion 中用 K_theory 和 K_empirical 的关系 frame 贡献，但 K_theory 的存在性从未被严格证明。\n- 建议：(1) 补充 sketch-level 数学推导；或 (2) 降低声称力度，明确 K* 是纯经验测量而非理论预测的验证。\n\n**N3** [critical] \"Domain as sole dominant factor\" 是过度声称\n\n- 位置：Section 4.5 (\"Domain as the sole dominant factor\"), Conclusion\n- (1) ANOVA (Table anova_crossscorer) 显示 Generator 的 η²_p = 0.885——这是一个很大的效应量，远非可忽略；(2) 仅 3 个 domain 无法支持排他性结论，domain 之间存在大量混杂变量（formatting, lexical diversity, topic diversity）；(3) Mistral-7B C4 K*=1 说明 model identity 确实影响 K*。\n- 建议：将 \"sole dominant\" 改为 \"primary\" 或 \"strongest observed\"，增加更多 domain 或明确 3-domain 的比较性结论限制。\n\n---\n\n### Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）\n\n**W1** [warning] Qwen 家族 5/9 配置导致聚合统计偏向\n- Table 1：\"8/9 configurations ~0.8pp，dose-response 显示低污染率下 synthetic data 是 net-beneficial。应明确 scope 为 proof of concept。\n\n**W3** [warning] Cross-model transfer matrix 不完整\n- Table S1 仅覆盖 4/9 配置，不含 [Round 1 审稿请求]:
## 交叉分享 — 请逐条回应 Reviewer 2 的审稿意见

以下是 Reviewer 2 的完整审稿原文。请对每个 Must-Fix 和 Should-Fix 逐条回应：
- **[AGREE]**：同意该问题存在，说明理由
- **[REBUT]**：不同意或认为问题程度不同，提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）

---

### Reviewer 2 的 Must-Fix 问题

**R2-N1** [critical] prompt-chain 与现实世界的 gap 未被充分讨论
- 所有主实验使用 prompt-chain，现实 web contamination 涉及 retrain-chain。Appendix retrain-chain K*≥4（远高于 K*=2），意味着 "binary filtering 即可" 的 prescription 可能在现实中失效；one-wash laundering 仅适用于 prompt-chain。核心 practical claim（K*=2 → binary filtering 最优）依赖 prompt-chain；若 retrain-chain K*≥4，graduated filtering 可能反而有效，直接颠覆 Section 5 结论。

**R2-N2** [critical] θ=0.60 的阈值选择缺乏独立验证
- θ=0.60 是 post-hoc 选择，存在 circular reasoning 嫌疑。K* 数值直接取决于 θ。θ=0.55 时部分配置 K*=3；θ=0.625 时 OLMo 降为 K*=1。建议 held-out domain/model family 独立验证或报告 θ-free 结果。

### Reviewer 2 的 Should-Fix 问题

**R2-W1** Qwen 家族占比过高（5/9 配置），headline claim 应改用 "4/5 architecture families"
**R2-W2** 下游 filtering 效果实际意义存疑（MMLU 仅 ~0.77pp），建议框定为 consistency check
**R2-W3** Cross-scorer 不稳定性未被充分反映（Qwen-cont cross-scoring K*: 2→1）
**R2-W4** arXiv K*=3 统计支撑较弱（n=2,200 < power curve 推荐 n≥2,500；cross-scorer 仅 2/4 CI-confirmed）

### Reviewer 2 的 Missing References
1. DivEye 与 15D 特征集重叠需更详细讨论
2. Synthetic data quality scoring 文献
3. Oren et al. (2024) contamination detection framework

### Reviewer 2 的其他发现
- Abstract 中 θ=0.60 和 AUC 未解释
- -ing participial trailing phrases 密度较高（~15 处）
- "First empirical measurement" 建议改为 "First systematic empirical characterization"
- 商业 API (GPT-5.5, Claude Opus 4.6) 可复现性问题

---

请逐条回应上述所有问题。

n≥2,500 的论据被夸大：power curve (Table power_curve) 针对的是 C4 上 K*=2 的 1v2 boundary，arXiv 的 2v3 AUC (0.62-0.65) 远高于 C4 的 1v2 AUC (0.61-0.70)，在 n=2,200 下统计功效可能更充分。直接套用不同 boundary pair 的 power curve 不恰当。\n\n但 cross-scorer CI 问题真实存在：Table arxiv_crossscorer_ci 显示 strict CI-confirmed 仅 2/4（Qwen-1.5B CI lower=0.617, LLaMA-8B CI lower=0.605 通过；Mistral-7B 和 Qwen-7B CI 跨越 θ）。论文自己承认了这一点（experiments_c1.tex L82）。\n\n结论：n 的问题不成立，但 cross-scorer robustness 问题是真实的。\n\n---\n\n### Missing References & 其他发现\n\n**[AGREE]** DivEye 特征重叠、synthetic data quality scoring 文献、Abstract 术语未定义、-ing phrases、商业 API 可复现性——均为合理指出。\n\n**[REBUT]** \"First empirical measurement\" → \"First systematic empirical characterization\"：K* 是本文首次定义的概念，\"first measurement\" 在定义上就是准确的。改为 \"systematic characterization\" 反而弱化贡献清晰度。\n\n---\n\n### 与我自己审稿意见的交叉对比\n\nR2 的 N1 (prompt-chain gap) 是我未独立提出的新问题，我同意其重要性。R2 的 N2、W1-W3 与我的 N1、W1-W2、W4 高度重合，说明这些是共识性问题。我的 N2 (DPI 论证不严密)、N3 (domain sole dominant factor overclaim)、W3 (transfer matrix 不完整)、W5 (one-wash 隐喻误导) 是 R2 未覆盖的独立发现。R2 的 W4 (arXiv 统计) 我部分反驳。\n\n两位 Reviewer 的共识 Must-Fix：θ 阈值的事后性（R1-N1 = R2-N2）。这是最需要作者 address 的问题。",

## PAGE 1

### Title
**Bounded Depth Discrimination in Recursive Synthetic Text: Measuring K* Across Domains, Architectures, and Scales**

### Authors
Anonymous submission

### Abstract

Web-scale training corpora are increasingly contaminated by machine-generated text that has itself been trained on earlier synthetic outputs, creating a recursive feedback loop of unknown depth. Current detection views this as a binary problem (human vs. synthetic) and ignores generational depth, leaving a critical question unanswered: at what recursive depth does synthetic text become statistically indistinguishable from its predecessors?

We introduce the *generational depth discrimination* framework and a new metric, *K\**, that a finite discrimination boundary *K\** must exist, beyond which *K*-th generation text is empirically indistinguishable from natural text under any classifier of bounded capacity. We formalize this as a direct consequence of the Data Processing Inequality (DPI): each generation is a stochastic transformation that can only lose mutual information with the original human distribution.

Our experiments span 9 model configurations across 4 families (GPT-Neo, LLaMA, Mistral, Qwen), 4 domains (news, code, fiction, scientific), and 2 scales (1.3B and 7B parameters). We find a consistent *K\** of *K\** = 1–3 (1v2 AUC drops below 0.6), with an empirical sweet spot at *K\** = 2 for most configurations. Domain structure modulates *K\**: Code yields the lowest *K\** (ceiling = 2) due to its rigid syntactic constraints, while Fiction shows the highest *K\** (up to 3) driven by broader stylistic variation. Scale trends show a binary detection (0v1) AUC decrease from 0.997 (1.3B) to 0.964 (14-32B), confirming that larger models produce more natural-sounding text, but this does not shift *K\** itself.

We show that *domain-aggregated K\** correctly predicts optimal data retention granularity. By connecting informat

Domain ceiling 框架具解释力和可证伪性；Mistral-7B 跨三域 K*={1,2,3} 是决定性证据。[idea]\nS2. 4 域 × 6+ 架构族交叉矩阵 + 32B scale 扩展，覆盖面强。[idea]\nS3. 统计方法规范（bootstrap CI, permutation test, JSD cliff ratio），boundary case 透明报告。[idea]\nS4. 1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
7	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
8	\label{fig:pipeline}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
12	\label{sec:formulation}
13	
14	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
16	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
17	Together with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\Ktheory$, so the actual saturation depth must be measured empirically.
18	
19	We define the operational lower bound:
20	\begin{equation}
21	  \Kempirical = \max\{k \geq 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k}) > \theta\},
22	  \label{eq:kstar}
23	\end{equation}
24	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
25	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise.
26	$\Kempirical$ characterizes a discriminability regime at a given $\theta$; t
…[truncated 5722 chars]

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across multiple communities.
11	Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).
12	Membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
13	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
14	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
15	
16	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
17	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principle
…[truncated 2289 chars]

3

1	\section{$\Kempirical$-Consistent Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical{=}2$ predicts that binary curation should suffice for web text; we test this as a consistency check using prompt-chain documents (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, proof of concept).
5	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): no-filter, binary-ours (15D LightGBM), binary-Drayson~\citep{drayson2025}, graduated ($w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), surprisal, and DSIR~\citep{xie2023dsir}, fine-tuning via LoRA on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (51 runs; 100\% synthetic upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
6	
7	\begin{table}[t]
8	\centering
9	\small
10	\caption{Filtering performance (mean $\pm$ std, 5 seeds). Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}
11	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
12	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
13	\end{table}
14	
15	Binary-ours reduces MMLU degradation from $-$1.53\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\,pp ($p_{\text{corr}} < 0.05$; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}); the modest absolute improvement reflects the constrained dynamic range of a fully synthetic setting.
16	The strategy ordering is consistent with $\Kempirical{=}2$: binary exploits the one reliable boundary (0v1), while graduated strategies weight depths 2--5 using near-random classifier outputs, injecting noise; at $\alpha{=}0.9$, graduated matches binary on HellaSwag but remains worse on MMLU ($p_{\text{corr}} = 0.011$).
17	Cross-model replication on Pythia-1.4B confirms binary $>$ graduated (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}); graduated weighting is indistinguishable from no-filter at all contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
18	On arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly fails: the statistical features separating depths 2 and 3 do not produce differential downstream training signal, so $\Kempirical$ accurately measures discriminability but

11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Neither frontier LLMs nor alternative classifiers improve depth recovery beyond this boundary (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Appendix).
20	
21	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
22	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$).
23	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts, extending $\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
24

_source line 837 · 2026-05-19T13:05:17.505395+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text (8/9 configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv, with domain structure as the primary determinant of the saturation boundary.
5	For most domains, a single generation pass renders synthetic text indistinguishable from deeper recursive output, limiting the practical ceiling of depth-based detection to one or two generations.
6	These domain-specific boundaries imply that contamination detection and data curation should adopt domain-aware thresholds rather than uniform binary classifiers; our generator-independent features ($\rho > 0.90$) provide a practical basis for such filtering.
7	Future work should extend this characterization to larger-scale models, additional domains, and temporal tracking of $\Kempirical$ as both models and training corpora evolve.
8

_source line 842 · 2026-05-19T13:05:24.784943+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}
5	Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{bertrand2024stability, schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model, shi2025collapse}.
6	\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} propose surplexity, a single surprisal-based collapse indicator; our 15-dimensional feature set captures richer distributional structure and enables cross-model transfer.
7	\citet{amin2025escaping} show that weak-data curation can mitigate collapse, connecting to our filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
8	Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}; $\Kempirical = 2$ is therefore a conservative lower bound.
9	This literature establishes convergence but provides no per-text depth measurement, does not predict domain dependence of saturation (Wikipedia depth 1 vs.\ arXiv depth 3), and does not characterize the prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain gap ($\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\ $7$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
12	Binary detectors~\cite{gehrmann2019gltr, mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, yang2024dnagpt, hans2024binoculars, verma2024ghostbuster, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate; surveys~\cite{wu2023survey, tang2024science} consolidate this landscape but frame detection as binary.

Scorer is statistically significant ($p < .001$) but has a small effect size ($\eta^2_p = 0.19$) relative to Depth-Pair ($\eta^2_p = 1.00$) and Generator ($\eta^2_p = 0.89$), indicating that scorer choice has a detectable but practically minor influence on depth discrimination (Scorer $\eta^2_p$ is approximately 5$\times$ smaller than Generator and dwarfed by Depth-Pair). 64:\subsection{Largely Generator-Independent Transfer}
65:\label{sec:transfer}
69:\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
70:\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 6-class ordinal accuracy when trained on the row model and tested on the column model, divided by in-domain accuracy (retention rate). Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 90--92\%; Qwen-rewrite$\to$continuation: 61--63\%.}
71:\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
74:Continuation-mode classifiers transfer well across generators (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}; full matrix in Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}): Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retains 90--92\%, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$Pythia 84--85\%, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$OLMo 80--85\% (0v1 cross-model $\auc > 0.96$ for all pairs).
75:Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\%), consistent with reliance on mode-specific artifacts rather than generalizable depth features.
92:Qwen-rewrite diverges (\texttt{freq\_entropy} dominates), consistent with its poor cross-model transfer (61--63\%).
100:The Qwen-only elevation reflects self-familiarity artifacts that transfer poorly (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}); the 15D features yield consistent $\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.

系统性降级：\"sole\"→\"primary\"，\"guarantees\"→\"motivates\"，Section 5 → \"consistency check\"。论文现在的声称力度与证据基础匹配。\n\n---\n\n### Updated Weaknesses\n\n**Remaining weakness (from prior round):**\n\n**W-remaining [Minor]** Transfer matrix 仍仅覆盖 4/9 配置，不含任何 7B+ 模型。\"80-92% retention\" 的 claim 限定为 \"tested\" 但读者仍可能 over-generalize。Contribution (c) 中这一 gap 应在 Limitations transfer 不是论文核心 contribution。\n\n新增 Code 域（S7）和 LOFO validation（S6）是实质性的 empirical 和 methodological 改进，而非 cosmetic 修改。\n\n论文现在呈现为：定义清晰的新问题 + 覆盖面广的经验测量（4 域 × 5 族 × 4 decoding）+ 恰当 scope 的 claims + 规范的统计方法 + 透明的 boundary case 处理。这达到了 EMNLP Main conference 的水准。\n\n| 维度 | Round 1 | Round 2 | 变化理由 |\n|------|---------|---------|---------|\n| Soundness | 3.5 | 4.0 | LOFO 解决 θ 循环；DPI 降级为 motivation **[STILL_OPEN]**\n\nTable S1（appendix.tex L39-60）仍仅覆盖 4 配置（Qwen-rw, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen-cont）。未新增 Qwen-7B、Qwen-14B、LLaMA-8B、Mistral-7B 的 transfer 数据。\n\nIntroduction（L30）增加了 \"tested continuation-mode configurations\" 限定词，但 \"80-92% retention\" claim 的基础仍为 1B-1.5B 模型之间的 transfer。7B/8B/14B 模型（占实验配置的 4/9）是否保持类似 transfer 性能完全未知。由于 transfer 是 Contribution (c) 的一部分，这一 gap 仍值得注意。\n\n**R8-W5 Cross-scorer 不稳定性** → **[RESOLVED]**\n\n- experiments_c1.tex L79-81: Qwen-cont 明确标注为 \"most scorer-sensitive\"，CI lower 仅超 θ 0.2pp ✓\n- Table s13（appendix.tex L442-461）：完整 cross-scorer 结果，Qwen-cont 是唯一 disagreement ✓\n- experiments_c1.tex L79: 报告 22 cross-scorer 配置中 20 保持 K*，2 例外为 borderline ✓\n\n**R10-N1 McNemar false claim** → **[RESOLVED]**\n\n- introduction.tex L28: \"McNemar p > 0.05\"（正确方向：非显著 = 无差异）✓\n- discussion.tex L19: 同 ✓\n- McNemar 完整表格（appendix_extended.tex Table mcnemar_obd_baselines）：36 comparisons, 31 non-significant ✓\n\n**R10-N2 Code domain** → **[RESOLVED]**\n\nCode 域（Python/CodeSearchNet）作为第 4 域高质量地集成：\n- 5 models / 4 architecture families（Table code_crossmodel_kstar）✓\n- K*=2 for 4 models, K*=1 for Pythia-1.4B（capacity limitation, paralleling Mistral C4 K*=1）✓\n- Cross-scorer validation（Pythia scorer on Qwen code: Δ=-1.9pp, K*=2 preserved）✓\n- JSD cliff ratio 11.7-13.5×，与 C4 cliff structure 一致 ✓\n- n=5,000 样本量充足 ✓\n- Mistral-7B Code K*=2 提供最强 domain-dependence 证据（同模型跨 3 域 K*={1,2,3}）✓\n\n**R8-M2 Missing references** → **[RESOLVED]**\n\n- CORAL: related_work.tex L23（cao2020coral）✓\n- Frank & Hall: related_work.tex L23（frank2001ordinal）✓\n- Oren et al.: related_work.tex L19 ✓\n- LightGBM: method.tex L48（ke2017lightgbm）✓\n- DivEye: experiments_c1.tex L171, related_work.tex L12 ✓\n\n**R8-M4 Abstract AUC/θ + Neural probe 样本量** → **[RESOLVED]**\n\n- Abstract（main.tex L39）: \"θ=0.60 (AUC decision threshold)\" ✓\n- Neural probe（experiments_c1.tex L99）: \"5,000 texts per depth\" ✓\n\n---\n\n### 修改是否引入新问题\n\n**Minor new observations:**\n\n1. **Power curve 更新**：Table power_curve（appendix_extended.tex L356-372）现在显示 n≥2,000 即 100% detection rate（此前审稿时为 n≥2,500）。这与 Limitations item (1) 的 \"n≥2,000 suffices\" 一致。arXiv n=2,200 现在满足此标准，之前 Reviewer 2 提出的 n=2,200 < 2,500 concern 不再成立。\n\n2. **arXiv filtering null result 已正文报告**（experiments_c3.tex L18）：这是积极改进。论文给出了 insight：\"K* accurately measures discriminability but overestimates filtering utility\"，这是一个有价值的 negative finding。\n\n3. **Code domain Pythia CI upper = 0.603**（Limitations item 1）：极度 marginal（0.3pp above θ），但论文诚实报告了这一点。Mistral Code K*=2 也标注为 marginal（CI lower within 0.3pp of θ）。\n\n---\n\n### Updated Strengths\n\n在前一轮 S1-S5 基础上新增：\n\n**S6.** LOFO cross-validation 为 θ=0.60 提供了有意义的独立验证。这一方法论改进直接回应了最大的 methodological concern，使得 K* 数值的可信度大幅提升。\n\n**S7.** Code 域作为第 4 域的加入（4 家族 5 模型 + cross-scorer）在保持实验质量的同时将 domain coverage 从 natural language 扩展到 programming language，Mistral-7B 跨 3 域 K*={1,2,3} 是整篇论文最强的 single-model evidence。\n\n**S8.** Retrain-chain K*=7（两模型两架构族收敛）使 prompt-chain/retrain-chain 对比从单模型轶事（K*≥4）升级为有 cross-architecture 支撑的结论，显著增强了论文对现实世界 contamination 的讨论深度。\n\n**S9.** Claims 系统性降级：\"sole\"→\"primary\"，\"guarantees\"→\"motivates\"，Section 5 → \"consistency check\"。论文现在的声称力度与证据基础匹配。\n\n---\n\n### Updated Weaknesses\n\n**Remaining weakness (from prior round):**\n\n**W-remaining [Minor]** Transfer matrix 仍仅覆盖 4/9 配置，不含任何 7B+ 模型。\"80-92% retention\" 的 claim 限定为 \"tested\" 但读者仍可能 over-generalize。Contribution (c) 中这一 gap 应在 Limitations 中更显著标注。\n\n---\n\n### Updated Overall Assessment\n\n**Overall: 4.0 (Conference)**\n\n**升分理由（从 3.5 → 4.0）：**\n\n三个 Critical issues 全部 resolved：\n- θ=0.60 的 LOFO validation 实质性缓解了循环论证风险（R8-N1）\n- Prompt-chain 限定在四处显著标注，retrain-chain K*=7 有双架构支撑（R8-N2）\n- \"sole\" → \"primary\" 全文替换（R8-N3）\n\n所有 Warning issues 中 6/7 resolved，唯一 STILL_OPEN（W4 transfer matrix）严重性从 Warning 降为 Minor——claim 已加限定词，且 transfer 不是论文核心 contribution。\n\n新增 Code 域（S7）和 LOFO validation（S6）是实质性的 empirical 和 methodological 改进，而非 cosmetic 修改。\n\n论文现在呈现为：定义清晰的新问题 + 覆盖面广的经验测量（4 域 × 5 族 × 4 decoding）+ 恰当 scope 的 claims + 规范的统计方法 + 透明的 boundary case 处理。这达到了 EMNLP Main conference 的水准。\n\n| 维度 | Round 1 | Round 2 | 变化理由 |\n|------|---------|---------|---------|\n| Soundness | 3.5 | 4.0 | LOFO 解决 θ 循环；DPI 降级为 motivation |\n| Excitement | 3.5 | 3.5 | 不变（新颖性和影响力评估一致） |\n| Reproducibility | 4.0 | 4.0 | 不变 |\n| Overall | 3.5 | 4.0 | 方法论 concern 大幅缓解，Code 域增强证据 |",

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
7	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
8	\label{fig:pipeline}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
12	\label{sec:formulation}
13	
14	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
16	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
17	Together with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\Ktheory$, so the actual saturation depth must be measured empirically.
18	
19	We define the operational lower bound:
20	\begin{equation}
21	  \Kempirical = \max\{k \geq 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k}) > \theta\},
22	  \label{eq:kstar}
23	\end{equation}
24	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
25	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise.
26	$\Kempirical$ characterizes a discriminability regime at a given $\theta$; t
…[truncated 5722 chars]

C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Neither frontier LLMs nor alternative classifiers improve depth recovery beyond this boundary (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Appendix).
20	
21	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
22	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$).
23	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts, extending $\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
24

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across multiple communities.
11	Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).
12	Membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
13	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
14	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
15	
16	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
17	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principle
…[truncated 2289 chars]

_source line 868 · 2026-05-19T13:10:35.211075+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text (8/9 configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv, with domain structure as the primary determinant of the saturation boundary.
5	For most domains, a single generation pass renders synthetic text indistinguishable from deeper recursive output, limiting the practical ceiling of depth-based detection to one or two generations.
6	These domain-specific boundaries imply that contamination detection and data curation should adopt domain-aware thresholds rather than uniform binary classifiers; our generator-independent features ($\rho > 0.90$) provide a practical basis for such filtering.
7	Future work should extend this characterization to larger-scale models, additional domains, and temporal tracking of $\Kempirical$ as both models and training corpora evolve.
8

37	\begin{abstract}
38	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
39	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable) using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
40	Across five architecture families (1B--14B), four source domains (three text, one code), and four decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$ (AUC decision threshold), $\Kempirical{=}2$ for general web text (8/9 model configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code (Python, model-dependent), and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for structured academic text (arXiv).
41	A single additional generation pass suffices to render first-generation synthetic web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \emph{one-wash laundering} effect.

505	\subsection{Leave-One-Family-Out Threshold Validation}
506	\label{sec:theta_lofo}
507	
508	To validate $\theta{=}0.60$ without circular reasoning, we perform leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation: each fold removes one architecture family, derives $\hat{\theta}$ as the midpoint between $\min(\auc_{1v2})$ and $\max(\auc_{2v3})$ over the remaining families, then predicts $\Kempirical$ for the held-out models.
509	Table~\ref{tab:theta_lofo} reports results across all three domains.
510	
511	On C4, 8/9 held-out predictions agree with the original $\Kempirical$ assignment at $\theta{=}0.60$ (agreement: 89\%).
512	The single mismatch is Qwen-1.5B-RW (rewrite mode), whose 2v3 AUC of 0.5893 lies above the LOFO-derived $\hat{\theta}{=}0.5725$ but below the global $\theta{=}0.60$; this configuration is also the sole rewrite-mode model, the discrepancy is therefore attributable to mode rather than threshold instability.
513	The derived $\hat{\theta}$ values span $[0.5725, 0.6004]$, and $\theta{=}0.60$ falls within this range.
514	
515	ArXiv corroborates with 6/6 agreement (100\%), though the wide $\hat{\theta}$ range $[0.5735, 0.7296]$ reflects OLMo-1B's near-chance performance ($\auc_{1v2}{=}0.503$), which inflates the gap midpoint when OLMo is included in the training fold.
516	
517	Wikipedia yields only 1/6 agreement (17\%).
518	This failure is expected: most models produce near-chance 1v2 AUC on Wikipedia ($0.499$--$0.619$), so the gap between $\min(\auc_{1v2})$ and $\max(\auc_{2v3})$ is narrow or inverted, the gap-based threshold derivation is therefore invalid.
519	Wikipedia's $\Kempirical{=}1$ assignment is independently confirmed by the global $\theta{=}0.60$ criterion (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}); LOFO simply cannot validate this because the method requires a clear AUC discontinuity betwee

420	Surprisal (base) features dominate on arXiv (single-group AUC $= 0.809$; LOO drop $= 0.031$, the largest across all domain--group combinations), consistent with the persistent surprisal-distribution signal in structured academic text.
421	On C4 and Wikipedia, all LOO drops are small ($\leq 0.007$, within cross-fold standard deviation), no single feature group is therefore individually critical for the weaker low-$\Kempirical$ signal.
422	Removing surprisal derivatives increases AUC on C4 (+0.003) and Wikipedia (+0.004). This indicates partial redundancy with the base surprisal group in low-$\Kempirical$ domains.

424	\subsection{Classifier Choice: McNemar Test}
425	\label{sec:mcnemar_baselines}
426	
427	To verify that depth discriminability is driven by the 15D feature representation rather than classifier choice, we compare OBD-LightGBM against three baseline classifiers (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) on the depth-1 vs.\ depth-2 binary classification task using McNemar's test with continuity correction on 5-fold GroupKFold out-of-fold predictions (Table~\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).
428	Of 36 comparisons, 31 are non-significant ($p \geq 0.05$), and all five significant results concentrate in two model $\times$ domain conditions.
429	For LLaMA-8B on C4, all three baselines significantly outperform OBD ($p \leq 0.001$), likely reflecting LightGBM overfitting on the relatively noisy 8B-generated features rather than a systematic classifier advantage.
430	For Qwen-1.5B on Wikipedia, OBD outperforms both linear classifiers ($p < 0.001$) but not random forest ($p = 0.655$).

67	\paragraph{CI methodology note.}
68	\label{sec:retrain_ci_note}
69	The main pipeline reports bootstrap 95\% CIs (1{,}000 or 10{,}000 resamples), which are valid because each bootstrap sample preserves the GroupKFold document structure: resampling operates on documents, and all depths of a given document remain together.
70	In the retrain-chain setting, each generation's training corpus is the \emph{output} of the previous generation's fine-tuned model. This creates inter-generation dependencies that standard bootstrap resampling cannot preserve, and naive resampling would inflate AUC estimates.
71	We therefore report fold-based 95\% CIs for retrain-chain experiments, computed as $\bar{x} \pm 1.96 \cdot s / \sqrt{K}$ from the $K{=}5$ GroupKFold AUC values.
72	
73	\emph{Threshold sensitivity.}
74	Table~\ref{tab:theta_sensitivity} reports $\Kempirical$ across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.70]$ for representative configurations including arXiv and cross-scorer conditions.
75	Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.2\,pp; all ten boundary comparisons yield permutation $p < 0.001$.

1	\section{$\Kempirical$-Consistent Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical{=}2$ predicts that binary curation should suffice for web text; we test this as a consistency check using prompt-chain documents (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, proof of concept).
5	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): no-filter, binary-ours (15D LightGBM), binary-Drayson~\citep{drayson2025}, graduated ($w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), surprisal, and DSIR~\citep{xie2023dsir}, fine-tuning via LoRA on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (51 runs; 100\% synthetic upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
6	
7	\begin{table}[t]
8	\centering
9	\small
10	\caption{Filtering performance (mean $\pm$ std, 5 seeds). Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}
11	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
12	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
13	\end{table}
14	
15	Binary-ours reduces MMLU degradation from $-$1.53\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\,pp ($p_{\text{corr}} < 0.05$; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}); the modest absolute improvement reflects the constrained dynamic range of a fully synthetic setting.
16	The strategy ordering is consistent with $\Kempirical{=}2$: binary exploits the one reliable boundary (0v1), while graduated strategies weight depths 2--5 using near-random classifier outputs, injecting noise; at $\alpha{=}0.9$, graduated matches binary on HellaSwag but remains worse on MMLU ($p_{\text{corr}} = 0.011$).
17	Cross-model replication on Pythia-1.4B confirms binary $>$ graduated (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}); graduated weighting is indistinguishable from no-filter at all contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
18	On arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly fails: the statistical features separating depths 2 and 3 do not produce differential downstream training signal, so $\Kempirical$ accurately measures discriminability but

_source line 910 · 2026-05-19T13:13:34.528383+00:00_

421-On C4 and Wikipedia, all LOO drops are small ($\leq 0.007$, within cross-fold standard deviation), no single feature group is therefore individually critical for the weaker low-$\Kempirical$ signal.
422-Removing surprisal derivatives increases AUC on C4 (+0.003) and Wikipedia (+0.004). This indicates partial redundancy with the base surprisal group in low-$\Kempirical$ domains.
423-
424:\subsection{Classifier Choice: McNemar Test}
425:\label{sec:mcnemar_baselines}
426-
427:To verify that depth discriminability is driven by the 15D feature representation rather than classifier choice, we compare OBD-LightGBM against three baseline classifiers (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) on the depth-1 vs.\ depth-2 binary classification task using McNemar's test with continuity correction on 5-fold GroupKFold out-of-fold predictions (Table~\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).
428-Of 36 comparisons, 31 are non-significant ($p \geq 0.05$), and all five significant results concentrate in two model $\times$ domain conditions.
429-For LLaMA-8B on C4, all three baselines significantly outperform OBD ($p \leq 0.001$), likely reflecting LightGBM overfitting on the relatively noisy 8B-generated features rather than a systematic classifier advantage.
430-For Qwen-1.5B on Wikipedia, OBD outperforms both linear classifiers ($p < 0.001$) but not random forest ($p = 0.655$).
--
432-\begin{table*}[h]
433-\centering
434-\small
435:\caption{McNemar test $p$-values comparing OBD-LightGBM against baseline classifiers on the depth-1 vs.\ depth-2 binary classification task (5-fold GroupKFold, out-of-fold predictions). Classifier accuracy shown in parentheses. Significant results ($p < 0.05$) are \textbf{bold}; $^{*}p<0.05$, $^{**}p<0.01$, $^{***}p<0.001$.}
436:\label{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}
437-\resizebox{\textwidth}{!}{%
438-\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
439-\toprule

McNemar claim 证据链断裂（R10-N1 / R9-N1）\n\n**[AGREE] R2-R9-N1: McNemar 证据链确实断裂，应升级为 Should-Fix**\n\n我在 Round 9 报告中将 R10-N1 标注为 [PARTIALLY RESOLVED] 并新增 NEW-W1 指出报告缺口，但未充分审视数据方向性。R2 的分析更到位，我修正判定如下：\n\nR2 指出 Table S3c per-depth recall 在 depths ≥ 2 上：\n- OBD: (14 + 19 + 22) / 3 = **18.3%**\n- GPT-5.5: (38 + 24 + 8) / 3 = **23.3%**\n- Claude Opus 4.6: (28 + 10 + 37) / 3 = **25.0%**\n\n我已验证这组数据。**这不仅是报告缺口，而是潜在的 claim-evidence 矛盾**：\n\n1. Discussion L19: \"Neither frontier LLMs nor alternative classifiers **improve** depth recovery beyond this boundary (McNemar p > 0.05)\" — 暗示无方法做得更好\n2. 但 Table S3c 显示 LLMs 在 depth ≥ 2 的 recall **确实高于** OBD（GPT-5.5 depth-2 recall 38% vs OBD 14%，差距 24pp）\n3. 即使 McNemar test 在 5-class 全局对比上不显著（OBD 的 depth-0 优势 96% vs 77%/69% 补偿了深层劣势），depth-specific 分析讲述了一个不同的故事\n4. \"all methods fail\" (Table S3c caption) 与 GPT-5.5 在 depth-2 达到 38% recall 存在张力 — 38% 虽然远非完美，但显著高于 5-class random chance (20%)\n\nR2 的两个建议均合理：\n- **选项 A**: 补充 beyond-K* McNemar test（限定在 depths 2-4 上的 pairwise comparison）\n- **选项 B**: 改用 \"all methods achieve near-chance accuracy beyond K*\" 并注明 LLMs 在 individual depth bins 有略高但不稳定的 recall\n\n我倾向选项 B，因为 GPT-5.5 depth-4 recall 仅 8% 而 Claude depth-3 仅 10%，说明 LLM 的 depth-specific 表现高度不稳定（非系统性改善），但当前 \"neither... improve\" 措辞确实不够准确。\n\n**修改 R8-W5 判定**：我原判 [RESOLVED]，现同意 R2 判定 **[PARTIALLY RESOLVED]**，因 McNemar claim 横跨 cross-scorer 和 frontier LLM 两个不同比较，后者证据链断裂。\n\n---\n\n### 2. Excitement 评分差异\n\n**[AGREE] R2 的 Excitement 3/5 理由部分成立，但我认为 3/5 略低**\n\nR2 论点：K*=2 与 DPI 预测方向一致，属 confirmatory measurement。\n\n我同意 DPI 确实预测了某个有限 K* 的存在，且 \"信息退化很快\" 这一方向性结论并不 surprising。但 R2 的论点忽略了两个不能从 DPI 推导的 non-trivial 发现：\n\n1. **Domain ordering 的定量化**：arXiv (K*=3) > C4 (K*=2) ≥ Code (K*=1-2) ≥ Wikipedia (K*=1)。DPI 预测不了 domain 会如此显著地 dominate model architecture（Mistral 在 C4 上 K*=1 但在 arXiv 上 K*=3，同一模型跨两级），这是一个 empirically novel 的发现。\n2. **K* 的具体值**：DPI 只说 I(X;Y_k) 单调递减，不给收敛速率。K*=2（而非 5 或 10）意味着信息损失极其集中于第一代，这对 data curation 有直接 actionable implication。\n\n但我也承认：\n- Filtering improvement 确实 modest（-1.53pp → -0.77pp），降低了 practical excitement\n- 作为 measurement paper，excitement 天然低于 method paper\n\n**最终判定**：我将 Excitement 从 3.5 **下调至 3/5**，与 R2 一致。理由补充：domain ordering 是 novel contribution，但 modest practical impact 和 confirmatory 大方向限制了 excitement ceiling。\n\n---\n\n### 3. R2 新发现逐条回应\n\n**[AGREE] R2-R9-N1 [warning]: McNemar citation chain 断裂**\n如上述第 1 点所述，我完全同意。这是我的 NEW-W1 的更细致版本。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-R9-N2 [minor]: 4 个 bib entries OpenAlex mismatch**\n我未做引用核查，无法独立验证。但 bib mismatch 属于可快速修复的技术性问题，不影响审稿判断。如 R2 已通过 verify_references 工具确认，我接受其判定。\n\n---\n\n### 4. R2 的 Should-Fix 建议回应\n\n**Transfer matrix 补充 LLaMA-8B/Mistral-7B**\n[AGREE] 与我的 W4 判定一致。至少补充 LLaMA-8B（论文中 borderline 配置）的 transfer 数据。Mistral-7B 因 C4 上 K*=1 导致 depth chain 更短，transfer 意义较小但仍值得报告。\n\n---\n\n### 5. 修订后的总体评分\n\n经过交叉审议，我维持 **Overall: 4.0 (Conference)**，但调整子维度：\n\n| 维度 | Round 9 原判 | 交叉后修订 | 变化理由 |\n|------|-------------|-----------|---------|\n| Soundness | 4/5 | 3.5/5 | McNemar 证据链问题比我原判更严重（R2 指出数据方向性矛盾） |\n| Excitement | 3.5/5 | 3/5 | 同意 R2 的 confirmatory measurement 论点 |\n| Reproducibility | 4/5 | 4/5 | 不变 |\n| Clarity | 4/5 | 4/5 | 不变 |\n\nOverall 4.0 不变的理由：Soundness 下调被 \"McNemar 问题可快速修复\"（补表或改措辞）抵消；核心发现（K*=2, domain ordering）的证据体系在 McNemar 问题修复后仍然成立。\n\n### 综合待修复清单（两位 Reviewer 共识）\n\n1. **[Should-Fix, 共识]** McNemar claim 修复：补充 beyond-K* OBD vs LLM McNemar 表，或改用 \"near-chance accuracy\" 表述 + 注明 LLM depth-specific recall 不稳定\n2. **[Should-Fix, 共识]** Transfer matrix 扩展至 ≥ 7B 模型\n3. **[Should-Fix, R1]** Abstract transfer 限定词统一（添加 \"tested\"）\n4. **[Minor, R2]** 4 个 bib mismatch 修复",

1. **McNemar 证据链**：同意 R2 判定 STILL_OPEN。Table S3c 数据确认 LLMs 在 depths≥2 recall 高于 OBD（23-25% vs 18.3%），与 "neither... improve" 措辞存在矛盾。建议改措辞或补 McNemar 表。
2. **Excitement**：下调至 3/5，接受 R2 的 confirmatory measurement 论点，但补充 domain ordering 作为 non-trivial 贡献。

4. **两位 Reviewer 共识**：McNemar 修复 + Transfer 扩展为 should-fix 项。

[Round 1 审稿请求]:
你是 Reviewer 1，负责审查方法严谨性和实验完整性。

## Venue 信息
- **目标 venue**: EMNLP 2026 (ARR May 2026 cycle)
- **截止日期**: 2026-05-25
- **格式要求**: ACL long paper, 正文 8 页上限（不含参考文献和 Limitations/Ethics），double-blind，匿名化
- **评分标准**: ARR Overall Assessment: 5=Award, 4=Conference, 3=Findings, 2=Resubmit, 1=Do not resubmit

## 论文路径
`projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/main.tex`
（含 introduction.tex, related_work.tex, method.tex, experiments_c1.tex, experiments_c3.tex, discussion.tex, conclusion.tex, appendix.tex, appendix_extended.tex）

## 你的审查侧重：方法严谨性 + 实验完整性

请重点评估以下方面：

### 方法论
1. **K*_empirical 定义** (Eq. 1): 操作定义是否严密？CI lower bound > θ 的 criterion 是否合理？
2. **θ=0.60 阈值选择**: 是否有充分的理论/实证支撑？post-hoc 选择是否存在循环论证？LOFO validation 是否可信？
3. **OBD-LightGBM 分类器**: 超参选择是否合理？与其他分类器的对比是否充分？
4. **15D 特征设计**: 特征集的选择依据是否清晰？self_BLEU 的 leakage 问题是否妥善处理？
5. **Bootstrap CI 和 permutation test**: 统计检验方法是否正确？retrain-chain 和 prompt-chain 的 CI 方法差异是否合理？
6. **DPI 理论论证**: 从 DPI 到 finite K* 的论证链是否严密？

### 实验设计
1. **模型覆盖**: 5 families, 9 configs, 1B-14B — 是否足够？Qwen 5/9 的偏倚是否影响结论？
2. **Cross-model transfer**: 覆盖范围（≤1.5B, 4 configs）是否足以支撑 "80-92% retention" claim？
3. **Cross-scorer validation**: 22 configs 中 20 preserved — 异常值 (Qwen-cont, Qwen-7B arXiv) 是否被充分讨论？
4. **Cross-domain**: arXiv (n=2200) vs C4 (n=5000) 样本量差异是否影响可比性？
5. **Filtering 实验**: 100% synthetic setting 的生态效度？dose-response 结果是否充分？效果量是否足够 meaningful？
6. **Neural probe**: 仅 3 models, Qwen self-familiarity artifact 的讨论是否到位？
7. **Missing controls/ablations**: 有没有遗漏的关键对照实验？

### 统计可靠性
1. 边界情况（LLaMA-8B borderline, Mistral K*=1, Qwen-cont scorer-sensitive）是否被诚实报告？
2. Multiple testing correction 是否恰当？
3. 效果量 vs 统计显著性的区分是否清晰？

请按以下格式输出你的审稿意见：
1. **Summary** (2-3句)
2. **Strengths** (逐条)
3. **Weaknesses** (逐条，标注严重程度: Must-Fix / Should-Fix / Minor)
4. **Questions** (需要作者澄清的)
5. **Missing References** (如有)
6. **Overall Assessment** (用 EMNLP ARR 评分标准)

36	% === Abstract ===
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
39	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable) using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
40	Across five architecture families (1B--14B), four source domains (three text, one code), and four decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$ (AUC decision threshold), $\Kempirical{=}2$ for general web text (8/9 model configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code (Python, model-dependent), and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for structured academic text (arXiv).
41	A single additional generation pass suffices to render first-generation synthetic web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \emph{one-wash laundering} effect.

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across multiple communities.
11	Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).
12	Membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
13	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
14	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
15	
16	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
17	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principle
…[truncated 2327 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
7	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
8	\label{fig:pipeline}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
12	\label{sec:formulation}
13	
14	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
16	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
17	Together with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\Ktheory$, so the actual saturation depth must be measured empirically.
18	
19	We define the operational lower bound:
20	\begin{equation}
21	  \Kempirical = \max\{k \geq 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k}) > \theta\},
22	  \label{eq:kstar}
23	\end{equation}
24	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
25	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise.
26	$\Kempirical$ characterizes a discriminability regime at a given $\theta$; t
…[truncated 5722 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate generational depth estimation on nine model configurations spanning five architecture families:
5	Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-14B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B, all in continuation mode except where noted.
6	All experiments use the OBD-LightGBM classifier with the 15-dimensional feature set (Section~\ref{sec:method}), with 5{,}000 texts per depth level (generations 0--5; power analysis in Appendix~\ref{sec:power_curve}).

1	\section{$\Kempirical$-Consistent Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical{=}2$ predicts that binary curation should suffice for web text; we test this as a consistency check using prompt-chain documents (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, proof of concept).
5	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): no-filter, binary-ours (15D LightGBM), binary-Drayson~\citep{drayson2025}, graduated ($w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), surprisal, and DSIR~\citep{xie2023dsir}, fine-tuning via LoRA on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (51 runs; 100\% synthetic upper bound~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
6	
7	\begin{table}[t]
8	\centering
9	\small
10	\caption{Filtering performance (mean $\pm$ std, 5 seeds). Full $\alpha$ sweep in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.}
11	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
12	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
13	\end{table}
14	
15	Binary-ours reduces MMLU degradation from $-$1.53\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\,pp ($p_{\text{corr}} < 0.05$; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}); the modest absolute improvement reflects the constrained dynamic range of a fully synthetic setting.
16	The strategy ordering is consistent with $\Kempirical{=}2$: binary exploits the one reliable boundary (0v1), while graduated strategies weight depths 2--5 using near-random classifier outputs, injecting noise; at $\alpha{=}0.9$, graduated matches binary on HellaSwag but remains worse on MMLU ($p_{\text{corr}} = 0.011$).
17	Cross-model replication on Pythia-1.4B confirms binary $>$ graduated (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}); graduated weighting is indistinguishable from no-filter at all contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
18	On arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly fails: the statistical features separating depths 2 and 3 do not produce differential downstream training signal, so $\Kempirical$ accurately measures discriminability but

_source line 951 · 2026-05-19T13:54:56.183331+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}
5	Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{bertrand2024stability, schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model, shi2025collapse}.
6	\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} propose surplexity, a single surprisal-based collapse indicator; our 15-dimensional feature set captures richer distributional structure and enables cross-model transfer.
7	\citet{amin2025escaping} show that weak-data curation can mitigate collapse, connecting to our filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
8	Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}; $\Kempirical = 2$ is therefore a conservative lower bound.
9	This literature establishes convergence but provides no per-text depth measurement, does not predict domain dependence of saturation (Wikipedia depth 1 vs.\ arXiv depth 3), and does not characterize the prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain gap ($\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\ $7$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
12	Binary detectors~\cite{gehrmann2019gltr, mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, yang2024dnagpt, hans2024binoculars, verma2024ghostbuster, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate; surveys~\cite{wu2023survey, tang2024science} consolidate this landscape but frame detection as binary.
13	Recent work extends beyond binary labels to collaboration degree~\cite{cheng2025beyond}, contamination hierarchies~\cite{mehta2025beyond}, and multigenerator benchmarks~\cite{wang2024semeval}; robustness concerns arise under paraphrasing~\cite{sadasivan2023detect, krishna2023paraphrasing} and cross-mode transfer~\cite{ha
…[truncated 1366 chars]

17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Even frontier LLMs, despite stronger depth-0 identification, exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\% with high cross-depth variance (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix).
20	
21	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
22	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$).
23	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts, extending $\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We measure $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text (8/9 configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv, with domain structure as the primary determinant of the saturation boundary.
5	For most domains, a single generation pass renders synthetic text indistinguishable from deeper recursive output, limiting the practical ceiling of depth-based detection to one or two generations.
6	These domain-specific boundaries imply that contamination detection and data curation should adopt domain-aware thresholds rather than uniform binary classifiers; our generator-independent features ($\rho > 0.90$) provide a practical basis for such filtering.
7	Future work should extend this characterization to larger-scale models, additional domains, and temporal tracking of $\Kempirical$ as both models and training corpora evolve.
8

# ACL Rolling Review Form - Complete Structure

## Core Scoring Dimensions

**Soundness** (1-5 scale)
- 5: "Excellent: This study is one of the most thorough I have seen, given its type"
- 4: Strong with minor improvements possible
- 3: Acceptable with some points needing support
- 2: Poor with insufficient support for main claims
- 1: "Major Issues: This study is not yet sufficiently thorough to warrant publication"

**Excitement** (1-5 scale)
- 5: "Highly Exciting: I would recommend this paper to others and/or attend its presentation"
- 4: Exciting with mention-worthy content
- 3: Interesting but not standout
- 2: "Potentially Interesting: this paper does not resonate with me, but it might with others"
- 1: "Not Exciting: this paper does not resonate with me"

**Overall Assessment** (1-5 scale)
- 5: Consider for Award (top 2.5%)
- 4: Conference acceptance
- 3: Findings track suitable
- 2: Resubmit next cycle
- 1: Do not resubmit

## Additional Evaluation Fields

- **Reviewer Confidence** (1-5): Assesses certainty in evaluation
- **Reproducibility** (1-5): Evaluates whether results can be reproduced
- **Datasets** (1-5): Rates value of released datasets
- **Software** (1-5): Rates value of released software
- **Author Identity** (1-5): Tracks double-blind integrity

## Narrative Sections

- Paper Summary
- Summary of Strengths
- Summary of Weaknesses
- Comments/Suggestions/Typos
- Limitations and Societal Impact
- Ethical Concerns
- Best Paper Justification (if applicable)

_source line 964 · 2026-05-19T13:55:52.950486+00:00_

# ARR Reviewer Guidelines: Evaluation Criteria

## Core Review Components

ARR reviewers assess papers using three primary scores introduced in February 2025:

**Soundness Score**: Evaluates technical validity. The guidelines stress that "low soundness scores must be justified by the text of the review." Reviewers should identify major methodological faults or logical gaps to support lower ratings.

**Excitement Score**: Reflects personal research interest, described as "orthogonal to the assessment of soundness." This allows reviewers to distinguish between technically sound work that may lack personal appeal versus naturally engaging research.

**Overall Assessment**: A composite recommendation reflecting soundness, excitement, novelty, and impact potential. This determines venue-level suitability.

## What Reviewers Evaluate

The guidelines identify six primary review tasks:

1. **Adjust expectations** by reading papers twice—skim-reading first to assess research contribution, then in-depth reading for technical evaluation
2. **Be specific** with citations and line references rather than vague criticisms
3. **Check for common review issues** (17 heuristics addressing confirmation bias, novelty obsession, SOTA fixation)
4. **Identify common NLP paper problems** across methodology, artifact sourcing, experimental results, and general clarity
5. **Ensure scores reflect textual justification**
6. **Maintain professional tone**

## Findings vs. Conference Tier Distinction

The guidelines state: "All papers recommended for Findings and main conferences are expected to be sufficiently sound and reproducible, but you may consider a paper worthy of the main conference even if you personally are not excited about it."

Key differentiator: Main conference papers warrant higher novelty/impact, while Findings accepts sound contributions with narrower scope or impact.
